# Energy | North America

# Refreshing Estimates & Revisiting Sensitivities

We refresh ests for 1Q, latest '26 guides and oil price strip as of 5/20. Our '26 oil prod'n for oil E&Ps +0.6% on avg with most spending plans unchanged. We see post-conflict value and would opportunistically add exposure to Majors & high-quality E&Ps on de-escalation pullbacks.

# Key Takeaways

With 1Q results, most left '26 activity & spending unchanged (except for FANG, COP, PR), some slightly raised volume guides at similar capex due to efficiencies.   
FY26 oil prod'n for oil E&Ps rises by 0.6% on average (near consensus). Guidance implies \~40 kb/d exit-exit US shale growth for E&Ps (\~180 kb/d incl. majors).   
We estimate a median 2026 FCF yield of 15% for our oil E&P coverage at latest strip (full-year average \~\$88 WTI) and 9% for gas E&Ps (\~\$3.67 HH).   
Oil producers are pricing in long-run WTI of \~\$70, \~16% below the 12-month strip. Gas E&Ps reflect \~\$3.50 Henry Hub, near the forward curve.   
We retain our preference for Majors (most upside for XOM) and E&Ps with positive rate of change (DVN, PR, CHRD & CVE).

Capital discipline holds despite higher prices. As we highlighted last week (see Higher Oil, Flat Activity - Recapping 1Q), 1Q earnings brought generally constructive operational updates, with most operators keeping 2026 activity plans unchanged despite higher prices (except for FANG, COP, & to a lesser degree PR). Some also modestly raised production outlooks with no change to spending, citing efficiency gains. Despite strong results and ongoing disruption in oil markets, the group is down \~3% month-to-date. While there is a wide range of near-term outcomes, including potential downside risk with a formal peace deal, fully restoring production and replenishing inventories will take time, likely supporting prices well beyond the end of the conflict. We would opportunistically add exposure to Majors & high-quality E&Ps with positive rate of change on de-escalation pullbacks.

Refreshing estimates. With this note, we refresh estimates for 1Q results and updated 2026 guidance. Full-year production for oil E&Ps in our coverage moves \~0.6% higher on average (near consensus), while capex is roughly unchanged (near consensus). In addition, we are refreshing our price deck to reflect the oil & gas strip as of May 20, bringing our assumed FY 2026 WTI price to \~\$88, up from \~\$79 prior. Higher crude prices drive an 18% average increase in our '26 FCF estimates, while our refreshed 2026 EBITDA estimates are 10% above consensus. Intrinsically, oil E&Ps reflect \~\$70 long-run WTI, below the 12-month strip. Gas stocks are pricing in \~\$3.50 Henry Hub, near the 12-month forward price.

MS & CO. LLC

# Estimate Revisions

# APA

- Our 2Q oil production estimate moves to 212 kb/d from 219 kb/d (guidance: 212 kb/d), while total volumes fall to 406 kboe/d from 443 kboe/d (guidance: 405 kboe/d), aligning with new guidance that includes \~35 kboe/d of US curtailments.   
- FY26 adjusted oil and total production are roughly unchanged (+1% on average vs MSe prior), as we had already assumed lower Egypt volumes due to PSC impacts from higher prices. FY26 capex remains \~\$2.1 B.   
- Before any changes to benchmark prices in our price deck, our 2026 FCF estimate decreases by \~10% to just over \$2.1 B on higher 2026 cash tax guidance. Including the latest strip, our FCF increases to \$2.6 B, +10% vs our prior estimate.   
- For 2027, reported oil production moves 2% lower versus our prior estimate, reflecting sequential declines in Egypt oil volumes from 2Q26 levels. FY27 capex is now \~\$2.18 B (-4% vs MSe prior), as we assume US spending stays around \$1.3 B (vs \~\$1.39 B prior), flattish with 2026 levels.   
- Model changes decrease our PT by \$1 to \$42/sh. Including the latest oil deck, our PT moves to \$44.

# AR

- AR held its 2026 production guidance flat at 4.1 Bcfe with cadence unchanged (4.1 Bcfe in 2Q and 4.2 Bcfe average production in 2H26). Management maintains flexibility to grow volumes to \~4.5 Bcfe in 2027.   
- Capex is similarly unchanged and AR expects \$1 B of D&C spend with \$100 MM of land spend. We continue to model execution on AR's growth plan (adding \~\$200 MM of D&C above guidance) as the company discussed its plans to drill DUCs and make a decision on completion later this year. We assume \$60 - 80 MM of drilling costs split between 2Q and 3Q with the remainder of completion spend in 4Q, although this could be deferred to 2027 if conditions warrant.

# CHRD

- To align with the 2Q guide, our oil production increases from 155 kb/d to 164 kb/d (guidance 162.5 - 165.5 kb/d), while capex is rising +8% to \$426 MM (guidance \$410 - \$440 MM). We forecast 3Q oil volumes to hold roughly flat with 2Q (3Q MSe 165 kb/d vs 167 kb/d prior) before declining in 4Q (MSe unchanged at 159 kb/d), with capex stepping down sequentially throughout 2H26. The 2H26 production and capex cadence is in line with company expectations.   
- CHRD raised FY26 production guidance by 2 kb/d to 160 - 162 kb/d (new MSe of 161.5 kb/d vs 158 kb/d prior) while maintaining capex midpoint at \$1.4 B, reflecting strong 1Q execution, base-production optimization, and faster cycle times.   
- Before any changes to benchmark prices in our price deck, our forecasted 2026 FCF is rising \~13% to \~\$1.45 B driven by higher oil volumes and stronger oil realizations, as CHRD raised FY26 guidance for WTI premium to $(0.50) - \$0.50$ from $(1.50) - (2.50)$ . Including the latest strip, our FCF increases to \~\$1.7 B.   
- In 2027, we forecast flat y/y oil production of 162 kb/d (MSe prior 159 kb/d), with capex near the 2026 level of \~\$1.4 B (+3% vs MSe prior).   
- PT increases from \$168/sh to \$175/sh, reflecting our updated oil price deck.

# CNX

- CNX maintained its 2026 production range of 605 - 620 Bcfe as it continues to execute on its maintenance production plan. Management expects production to be roughly ratable from quarter-to-quarter this year.   
- Capex guidance was unchanged with 1Q results at \$556 - 586 MM and management continues to expect spending weighted towards 1H.   
- On the call, management discussed opportunities in the Utica and expects to continue developing this program. Current messaging suggests a more fulsome update on the play towards year-end. CNX sees a constructive picture for longer-term in-basin demand growth and is participating in RFPs (request for proposals) for gas supply.

# COP

- We previously published estimate revisions on 5/1 for the 1Q earnings release (see here):   
- Increased FY26 capex by \~\$260 MM to the midpoint of COP's revised guidance range of \$12.0-\$12.5 B (from \$12 B prior) due to higher Permian activity.

• Decreased 2Q total production by 2% to 2,221 kboe/d mostly driven by 24 kboe/d less volumes from Qatar as COP excluded all volumes from the country (80 kboe/d implied run rate) from quarterly guidance (2,185-2,215 kboe/d). Our FY26 estimate is unchanged and in line with COP's revised guidance range of 2,295-2,325 kboe/d.

# CRK

- Despite soft 1Q results, CRK maintained production guidance for 1,250 - 1,400 MMcfe/d of volume this year. We continue to model the company returning to 1H24 levels by 4Q (\~1.5 Bcfe/d) with a ramp throughout 2026, but are in the lower half of the full-year production range.   
- Capex guidance for \$1,400 - 1,500 MM was unchanged with 1Q earnings and CRK continues to expect 5 rigs in the Legacy Haynesville and 4 in the Western Haynesville.   
- PT from \$19/sh to \$18/sh, reflecting our updated gas price deck.

# CVE

- Reduced buyback allocation in 2Q by C\$260 MM to C\$600 MM, consistent with management comments of cash allocation having a bias towards deleveraging in the current price environment.   
- Excluding the increase in our 2026 price deck, our FY26 FCF estimate moves 9% higher mostly due to CVE reporting 1Q cash flow roughly C\$500 MM higher than our prior estimate.

- Management expects delivery of West White Rose in late 3Q vs prior messaging of late 2Q. As a result, we slightly push back first oil of the project, driving \~4 k/bd lower production to Atlantic volumes in 2H26. Our FY26 Atlantic production of 21 kb/d is still within the guidance range of 20-25 kb/d.   
• PT from C\$41/sh to C\$43/sh.

# CVX

\- We previously published estimate revisions on 5/4 for the 1Q earnings release (see here):

• We increased equity affiliate distributions in 2Q (consistent with \$2.5 B guidance) and assumed higher payouts in 2H26 due to higher oil prices. The total impact is a \~\$1 B uplift to our cash flow estimates and a \~3% increase in FY26 FCF (excluding commodity price assumptions), relative to our prior forecast.   
- Total 1Q incremental timing effects came in at \~\$2.9 B, \$300 MM lower than our estimate. As a result, we lowered total timing effect reversals by this amount for the remainder of year (no net impact to FY26 estimates).   
- Decreased 2Q26 International refining utilization by 19% to \~80% in 2Q26 (in line with guidance), lowering our total throughput by 7% to 1,554 kb/d.

# DVN

- With this update, we incorporate the CTRA merger, which closed on May 7. For 2026, we assume \~2% combined oil production growth based on DVN standalone maintenance program and \~5% annual growth for CTRA.   
- We assume full \~\$1 B synergies by 2027, including \~\$350 MM from capital optimization, \$350 MM from operating margin improvements, and \$300 MM from lower corporate costs.   
- On cash tax, we model a blended rate (MSe \~18% in 2026) of CTRA (20-25%) and DVN (15-17%) standalone cash tax rates.

# EOG

- Our 2Q oil production estimate is decreasing from 551 kb/d to 549 kb/d and capex is rising \~3% to \$1,628 MM to align with guidance. In 2H, we forecast average oil volumes of \~550 kb/d (vs 545 kb/d prior) with a slightly lower gas production relative to prior estimate (though still increasing throughout 2026), as EOG is reallocating some gas spend from Dorado to oilier assets in the back half of the year.   
- In 2027, our oil production is unchanged at 553 kb/d while gas volumes increase slightly (<2% vs prior estimate) driven by assumed higher Utica activity.   
- We forecast broadly unchanged capex of \~\$6.5 B in 2026 and \~\$6.6 B in 2027.   
- On shareholder returns, we now assume \~70% of 2026 FCF is returned via base dividends and buybacks (vs 95% FCF prior), as EOG signaled a more measured buybacks payouts in the current price environment.   
- PT increases from \$155/sh to \$160/sh, reflecting our updated oil price deck.

# EQT

• We published estimate revisions on 4/22 subsequent to 1Q26 earnings (see here):   
• EQT initiated 2Q production guidance of 570 - 620 Bcfe which includes 10-15 Bcfe of strategic curtailments. Capex of \$735 - 830 MM includes \$525 - 595 MM of maintenance spending with the remainder tied to strategic growth.   
- EQT reiterated its 2026 guidance ranges for 2,275 - 2,375 Bcfe of production and \$2,650 - 2,850 MM of capex – implying a sequential step-down for both vs. 2Q peak levels. We believe the implied 2H26 production guide appears conservative and model the high end of the range accordingly.   
• Data center and LNG strategy were topical on the earnings call. EQT sees 6-7 Bcf/d of in-basin demand growth by 2030 and has LNG offtake agreements starting up in 2030-31 that effectively link 10-15% of EQT's total production to international pricing.

# EXE

• We published estimate revisions on 5/1 subsequent to 1Q26 earnings (see here):   
• 2026 production and capex estimates are largely unchanged as EXE reiterated plans to produce \~7.5 Bcf/d with \~\$2.85 B capex. We continue to model 1H production <7.5 Bcf/d and 2H output above that level.   
- Increased 2Q capex by 16% to align with guidance of \$770-\$845 MM, the high point of the year. EXE shifted some activity to 2Q due to weather-related deferral, higher leasehold acquisition as well as a seasonal pick-up in workover activity.

# FANG

• We published estimate revisions on 5/6 subsequent to 1Q26 earnings (see here):   
• Our 2Q-4Q production estimate remains roughly unchanged, as our forecasts already reflected DUC draws. We estimate 2026 oil production of 525 kb/d, about +2% vs prior MSe (including stronger 1Q actuals).   
• We continue to estimate capex at \~\$3.9 B in both 2026 and 2027.   
• We now assume no buybacks in 2Q with share repurchases resuming at 20% FCF payout in 2H26 (vs \~65% FCF prior), rising to 50% FCF in 2027+.

\- PT increases from \$224/sh to \$229/sh, reflecting our updated oil price deck.

# IMO

- Following unplanned downtime at Syncrude in 1Q, we shifted the planned turnaround (4 kb/d annualized impact) at the plant from 2Q to 3Q, consistent with management messaging. We forecast FY26 Syncrude production at the bottom end of the asset's guidance range of 78-82 kb/d.   
- We increase gross refining margin per barrel 14% to C\$57.25 reflecting higher market capture assumptions after strong historical performance relative to our estimates. This change more than offsets the large 1Q cash flow miss vs our prior forecasts (transitory unfavorable deferred tax impacts) before our commodity price refresh for 2026.   
- FY26 refining throughput decreased by 2% to 394 kb/d, in line with the bottom end of the guidance range (395-405 kb/d) due to lower-than-expected 1Q throughput at Strathcona stemming from the aforementioned Syncrude downtime in the quarter.

# MTDR

- We are increasing our estimate for 2Q oil production from 123 kb/d to 124 kb/d (guidance 123 - 125 kb/d), while total volumes decrease by 2% to 210 kboe/d, reflecting 10 kboe/d of expected curtailments during the quarter. 2Q capex estimate is moving <1% lower to \$448 MM to align with the guidance (\$430 - 460 MM).   
- Our oil volume estimates increase to 126 kb/d in both 3Q (vs 124 kb/d prior) and 4Q (vs 123 kb/d prior), while average quarterly capex in 2H remains broadly unchanged at \~\$314 MM, below the 2Q guidance range, consistent with company expectations.   
- MTDR raised its 2026 oil outlook (to 123 – 125 kb/d from 122 – 124 kb/d) but maintained capex guide (\$1.45 – \$1.55B), reflecting efficiency gains.   
- Before any changes to benchmark prices in our price deck, our 2026 free cash flow (FCF) decreases 5% vs prior MSe as we now forecast lower FY26 hedged gas realizations (\$1.94/mcf now vs \$3.00/mcf before), as MTDR guided to a \$4-5/mcf discount to Henry Hub bidweek in 2Q. Including the latest oil strip, our FCF increases to \$1.15 B, +20% vs our prior estimate.   
- In 2027, our FCF increases 8% to \$942 MM driven by higher oil production (MSe from 125 kb/d to 126 kb/d) and lower capex (\~\$1.51 B vs \$1.54 B prior), reflecting 2026 efficiencies.   
- PT increases from \$73/sh to \$75/sh, reflecting our updated oil price deck.

# MUR

- Our 2Q oil volumes increase from 81 kb/d to 86 kb/d (guidance \~86 kb/d) driven by 1Q production outperformance in Eagle Ford and Gulf of America (GoA). Our 2Q capex is increasing +17% vs MSe prior to \$390 MM to align with the new guidance (\$350 – \$430MM), reflecting a heavier 1H onshore D&C program and exploration/appraisal spend in Vietnam and Côte d'Ivoire.   
- We lower our oil production estimate by \~2 kb/d to 85 kb/d in 3Q and by \~1 kb/d to 90 kb/d in 4Q. The 4Q volumes still imply +5% q/q growth, as the reversal of assumed storm downtime and the Chinook #8 well start-up more than offset onshore production declines, with no 4Q wells planned in Eagle Ford, Kaybob Duvernay, or Tupper Montney.   
- For FY26, our oil production is increasing by 1% to 87 kb/d at unchanged capex of \~\$1.25 B.   
- Our 2027 oil production estimate is decreasing by 5% to 93 kb/d as we assume a smoother production ramp from the Lac Da Vang development in Vietnam expected to come online in 4Q26.   
- We also assume that MUR's LOE (lease operating expense) remains in the \$10-12/boe range going forward as 1Q26 was below typical range temporarily due to in-year cost phasing.   
- As a result, our 2027 free cash flow (FCF) is moving lower relative to prior estimate.

# NOG

- With earnings, NOG reiterated its two guidance ranges for low and high commodity prices. We assume oil production of 73 MMbbl/d in 2Q and 3Q (vs 1Q of 74) with an uptick in 4Q to 75 as AFE (authorization for expenditure) elections and production decisions made near term will impact volumes into 4Q and 2027.   
- We continue to model spending between low and high guidance cases and assume the high end of the low range of guidance for 2Q-3Q with the quarterly midpoint of the high range for 4Q.   
- We expect NOG to collapse its guidance ranges once greater visibility is gained into production cadence and spending needs.

\- PT from \$28/sh to \$29/sh, reflecting our updated oil price deck.

# PR

- Increased 2Q and 2026 oil production by 2% to align with guidance (FY 190-195 kb/d oil), driven by increased workovers, acceleration of completions and reduced down time.   
- Increased 2Q and 2026 capex by 5% and 3%, respectively, reflecting management messaging of upper half of the full-year range.

# RRC

• We published estimate revisions on 4/22 subsequent to 1Q26 earnings (see here):   
- Increased 2Q and 3Q capex by \~4% on average, due to the addition of a spot completion crew to work through drilled but uncompleted (DUC) inventory (\~65-70% liquids rich).   
• Our 2026 production and capex estimates are largely unchanged. We continue to model back-half-weighted volume tied to new processing capacity and utilization of DUCs.   
• 2026 FCF estimate is rising driven by better NGL realizations (\~\$2/bbl premium to Mont Belvieu vs \~\$1.13/bbl prior).

# SU

- Shifted roughly 30 kb/d of oil volumes from 3Q to 2Q reflecting the postponement of the planned turnaround at Syncrude (35 kb/d negative impact to bitumen and 30 kb/d impact to SCO and diesel production). We now forecast Syncrude production at the low end of the '26 guidance range of 200-210 kb/d. Our FY26 total production is roughly unchanged.   
- Added \$1 B operating working capital tailwind in 2Q following a \$1.6 B headwind in 1Q from the strong positive move in commodity pricing in March (a lot of this has already been released according to management).

# TOU

- Our 2026 and 2027 production and capex estimates are largely unchanged as TOU reiterated its multi-year growth plan.   
- We lowered 2Q production by 1% to align with guidance of \~600 kboe/d. We continue to model similar levels in 3Q and peak in 4Q driven by higher activity and the expected completion of the Aitken project.

# VNOM

• We published estimate revisions on 5/6 subsequent to 1Q26 earnings (see here):

- We increase our 2Q oil production estimate by $1\%$ to 65 kb/d to align with VNOM's 2Q guide of 64 – 65 kb/d.   
VNOM raised standalone FY26 net oil production guidance by 2% at midpoint to 64.5 – 66.5 kb/d, driven primarily by FANG's acceleration of near-term activity. We also incorporate a pending Riverbend acquisition, expected to close in early 3Q26, which should add 4 kboe/d of production (2 kb/d for oil) over the next 12 months, or +1 kb/d to VNOM's standalone FY26 oil guide. As a result, our oil volumes rise to 67 kb/d (+5% vs MSe prior) in 2026 and to 72 kb/d (+11% vs MSe prior) in 2027.

# XOM

\- We previously published estimate revisions on 5/4 subsequent to XOM's 1Q earnings release (see here):

- Lowered 2Q production by 6% to the midpoint of XOM's guidance range (4.1-4.3 mboe/d), driven by exclusion of UAE and Qatar volumes, a \~320 kboe/d combined impact vs our prior estimate. For the full year vs our prior forecast, this impact is partially offset by a quicker recovery time from the Strait reopening and from higher estimated US base volumes.   
• Decreased the positive timing effect reversal for the remainder of the year in the Energy Products segment by \~\$500 MM, as 1Q negative timing effects came in at \~\$3.4 B vs our previous estimate of \~\$3.9 B. We assume a 75%/25% international/US split for 1Q Energy Products timing effects, and that these

impacts fully reverse across 2Q and 3Q.

- Increased equity affiliate distributions for the remainder of the year by C\$500 MM, reflecting higher assumed payouts from higher oil prices.   
- Added \$2 B working capital headwind in 2Q for seasonal cash tax payments per XOM.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

■ High quality company at a premium valuation. CNQ has the most leverage to higher oil prices in our Canadian coverage and is a low-cost producer. CNQ trades at 8% FCF, slightly higher than the peer average (7%), while EV/DACF is roughly in-line.   
■ Net debt reduction progression, accelerating shareholder returns. After reaching C\$16 B of net debt as of the 1Q26 earnings release, CNQ will now return 75% of FCF to shareholders (up from 60%). We forecast '27 total shareholder yield of 7%, in-line with the peer average.

# BULL CASE

# 9.6x 2027e DACF of C\$24 B

Bull case of C\$104/sh which is a 50/50 blend of 1) a target multiple of 8.0x 2027e EV/DACF (C\$113/share) and 2) our discounted cash flow value of C\$95/share (with a WACC of 7.7% and terminal EV/EBITDA multiple of 6.5x). Assumes long-term (2027+) prices of US\$95.00 Brent, US\$4.25 HH, refining cracks increase \~10%, carbon prices flat from 2022-2030.

# C\$104.00

# BASE CASE

# 8.6x 2027e DACF.

Base case of C\$67/sh which is a 50/50 blend of 1) a target multiple of 7.5x 2027e EV/DACF (C\$72/share) and 2) our discounted cash flow value of C\$62/share (with a WACC of 7.4% and terminal EV/EBITDA multiple of 5.7x). Assumes long-term (2027+) prices of US\$75.00 Brent, US\$3.75 HH, WTI-WCS of US\$15, carbon prices increase C\$15/tonne per year through 2030.

# BEAR CASE

# C\$26.00

# 6.4x 2027e DACF of C\$11 B

Bear case of C\$18/sh which is a 50/50 blend of 1) a target multiple of 7.5x 2027e EV/DACF (C\$24/share) and 2) our discounted cash flow value of C\$13/share (with a WACC of 7.7% and terminal EV/EBITDA multiple of 3.1x). Assumes long-term (2027+) prices of US\$50.00 Brent, US\$3.25 HH, refining cracks decline \~10%, carbon price increases by C\$30/tonne per year through 2030.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

■ COP checks all the boxes for sustained outperformance: excellent management, disciplined investment, and consistent return of cash coupled with a high quality, low cost portfolio that can deliver an attractive combination of FCF and shareholder returns.   
■ Strong growth ahead. Delivery of long term projects (Alaska, Qatar, and Port Arthur LNG) underpin an impressive FCF CAGR of 14% through the rest of the decade.   
■ Resilient to lower commodity prices. COP's strong cash balance provides financial flexibility and resiliency to lower prices while its unhedged exposure allows for upside participation.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

- Attractive valuation. Stock underperformed Canadian peers by \~12% since the start of 2024, tied in part to weak refining performance. With a leading in-situ production portfolio, CVE screens cheap with an EV/DACF of 6.5x in '27, lower than their Canadian peer average of 8.8x.   
■ FCF inflection. Delivery and ramp-up of CVE's large slate of upstream projects drives 11% FCF yield in 2026 (vs 10% from peers), and 8% in 2027 (7% from peers).

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

A peer-leading balance sheet and strong downstream assets (3 refineries spanning eastern and western Canada) should allow the company to continue its strong cash flow generation. However, we note high capex in 2026-29 (\~C\$1.9 B) limits FCF upside. Additionally, the stock trades at a premium vs Canadian peers, which we think fairly prices in the advantaged balance sheet and downstream assets on a relative basis.

■ IMO is still in the early stages of its new energies investment, but we note the renewable diesel facility at Strathcona (20 Mbbl/d capacity) provides leverage to the upside.

■ IMO trades at a 2027 EV/DACF of 11.1X, well above the peer average of 7.3X.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

■ HES strengthens outlook. Through its acquisition of HES, CVX gains exposure to rising high margin volumes in Guyana, driving a 1.5% bump in the company's oil production CAGR for the remainder of the decade.   
■ Strong rate of change into 2026. By 2026, CVX expects \$12.5 B of FCF uplift from TCO, GoA, Permian, cost cutting initiatives, and HES. This leads to a FCF yield of 11% in '26 (at \$88 WTI).   
■ Durable FCF growth. With its '25 Investor Day, CVX highlighted a >10% FCF CAGR through 2030 underpinned by a low-risk, high-confidence set of investment opportunities.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

Valuation. Since the start of 2024, SU has outperformed Canadian peers \~25% on the back of a series of strong quarterly results, and higher shareholder returns. We now estimate '27 FCF yield of \~7%, in-line with the peer average.   
■ More growth and visibility. The '26 investor day outlined C\$2 B of FFF growth (at \$65 WTI) through 2028 and a \$5 dollar reduction in WTI breakeven. SU also provided visibility to long term bitumen supply options into next decade.   
■ Shareholder returns. SU trades at a 7% 2027E total shareholder return yield, higher than the peer average of 6%.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

- Low cost of supply relative to peers.
FANG's 2026 FCF breakeven price of \~\$39 (post-base dividend), one of the lowest in our coverage, fortifying FANG as one of the lowest-cost producers in the Permian.   
■ Strong FCF generation through the cycle. We estimate FANG's FCF yield of \~13% in '26 at \~\$88 WTI, above peers' median, driven by strong capital efficiency gains, Endeavor synergies trending ahead of plan, and FCF accretion from the Double Eagle acquisition.   
■ Focus on lower leverage and strong shareholder returns. While FANG's consolidated net debt is expected to increase post the Double Eagle deal and VNOM/Sitio merger, we expect net debt to fall <\$10 B by YE26 at prices near strip.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

■ We estimate VNOM to trade in-line with oil E&Ps on 2027 FCF yield (10% for VNOM vs 11% for oil E&Ps) and shareholder returns (12% yield for VNOM vs 6% for oil E&Ps).   
■ Above average multi-year growth outlook at no D&C capex risk. VNOM offers a superior oil production growth relative to oil E&Ps at zero drilling and completion (D&C) capital requirement. We estimate the proforma CAGR for oil per debt adjusted share of 11% in 2025-28 for VNOM vs 8% for oil E&Ps.   
■ Superior EBITDA margins. Viper generated one of the highest 2026 EBITDA margins among mineral peers due to its lower cost structure and oil-weighted production mix.

# UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

■ Further upside hinges upon successful de-risking of future Suriname developments. While Suriname reached its first FID, we believe more extensive exploration and appraisal work is required to de-risk future potential phases.   
■ Challenges in operational assets. APA plans to cease operations in the North Sea (due to unfavorable regulatory regime & high taxes) well ahead of the original timeline. In addition, we continue to see a challenged long-term outlook for gas-rich Alpine High.   
■ Targeting \$450 MM in annual cost savings by YE26 in capital, LOE, and overhead. While this is a positive development, investors need to see clear progress toward these goals before giving credit to stronger FCF

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

■ Integrated midstream operations provide a competitive advantage. Besides providing MTDR with flow assurance, San Mateo Midstream JV (MTDR 51% stake) services third-party customers and generates positive cash flow. We estimate San Mateo to add \~\$10/sh of value to MTDR's NAV.   
■ Modest shareholder returns at in-line valuation. We estimate MTDR to trade in-line w/ peers on '26 EV/EBITDAX and offer a \~3% return yield in '26 vs 6% for peers.

■ Exposure to Haynesville and Bossier shale regions in LA and TX provide access to growing LNG fairway.   
■ Exploratory Western Haynesville opportunity offers long-term growth, but economics and productivity remain unclear.   
- Jerry Jones, owner of the Dallas Cowboys Football Team, holds majority stake in CRK (71%).   
■ While FCF/equity improves from -5% in '26 to -1% in '28, CRK lags peers.

# UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

■ Unique business model of non-operated oil & gas positions differs vs peers as NOG has interests in the Permian, Williston, Appalachian, and Uinta regions.   
■ Consistently raised dividends under a program initiated in 2021 and increased authorization in early 2025, but moderated growth expected.   
■ We see risk of further non-operated activity reductions in the event oil prices move lower.   
■ NOG has a lower FCF yield than peers in 2026 with higher leverage (1.6x vs oil median of 0.4x).

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

\- Pending merger with CTRA would create the second largest US independent E&P by total volume and is \~17% accretion to FCF/sh for DVN at \$60 WTI and \$3.75 HH.
- DNV is on track to deliver the full \$1 B business optimization FCF uplift with the upcoming \$1 B term loan maturing September '26, ahead of schedule and offers attractive non-commodity linked rate of change.

\- At \$70 WTI, we estimate DVN offers a 13% FCF yield d in 2027, above the oil E&P median of 11% and large-cap peers of 9%, respectively.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

■ OXY is differentiated by its high quality assets and LT growth potential through Low Carbon Ventures. That said, FCF/Equity is modestly below oil E&P average.

\- OxyChem sale supports balance sheet, but reduces FCF. The transaction is dilutive to our 2026-27 FCF/sh estimates, but accelerates debt reduction (0.5x lower pro-forma leverage) and pulls forward shareholder returns.

■ Free option on low carbon growth. OXY a leader in Direct Air Capture and has been active in helping hyperscalers decarbonize, including a recent 500,000 tonne deal with Microsoft. The stock reflects zero value for its low carbon business.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

In-line valuation and capital efficient growth. RRC offers a 2026 FCF yield of 9%, in line with the peer median, with above-average growth over the next few years.   
■ Access to export markets supports premium NGL realizations. The company has realized a >\$1.50/bbl premium to Mont Belvieu over the last few years, a trend we expect to continue.   
■ Shareholder returns near peers. RRC offers a total return yield of \~2%, near the peer median. The company has an opportunistic approach to share buybacks and has low leverage.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

■ EXE is the largest independent natural gas producer in the US, well positioned to answer the call on the next growth cycle of gas supported by a diverse transportation portfolio and direct access to the Gulf Coast LNG corridor. Marketing & commercial segment could drive incremental value longer term.   
■ Rate of change from synergies realization. EXE offers attractive FCF growth from rising prices and volumes in 2026. We estimate a 12% FCF yield in 2026, above peer median of 9%.   
■ Capital returns framework designed to return cash and reduce net debt. We estimate a total return yield of 6% in 2026, above the gas E&P median of 4%.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

■ Enhanced scale in the Bakken at attractive valuation. We estimate CHRD trades at a discount to its peers on '26 EV/EBITDAX.   
- CHRD offers strong FCF generation across a range of oil prices and at \$88 WTI, we see a 21% FCF yield (oil E&P average of 15%) and 15% shareholder return yield (peer average of 6%).   
We expect CHRD to continue realizing capital efficiency gains and see a positive rate-of-change on its longer lateral program. For 2026, CHRD expects 80% of its planned wells to be 3- to 4-mile laterals (vs. \~45% last year).   
■ While CHRD's net leverage ticked up post the XTO Bakken acquisition, we estimate it returns below 0.5x by YE26 at \$88 WTI.

# STOCK RATINGS

MS uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). MS does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of all ratings used in MS. In addition, since MS contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read MS, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

# Global Stock Ratings Distribution

(as of April 30, 2026)

The Stock Ratings described below apply to MS's Fundamental Equity Research and do not apply to Debt Research produced by the Firm.

For disclosure purposes only (in accordance with FINRA requirements), we include the category headings of Buy, Hold, and Sell alongside our ratings of Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight. MS does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold, and sell but represent recommended relative weightings (see definitions below). To satisfy regulatory requirements, we correspond Overweight, our most positive stock rating, with a buy recommendation; we correspond Equal-weight and Not-Rated to hold and Underweight to sell recommendations, respectively.

# Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in MS is 12 to 18 months.

# Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
